===== default feasible True
 step 1 | Inside the core, uranium nuclei in the TRISO fuel particles split when they absorb a neutron, and the graphite slows the new neutrons so the chain reaction carries on. The fission heat passes to helium flowing through the core, which leaves at 925°C. The reactor runs at 178.5MWth of its 210MWth rating.
   notes: ['The design basis is a generic 925°C HTGR. No commercial reactor runs at this outlet, and Xe-100 is only a labelled analogue with a much lower outlet temperature.']
   figs: {'rot': ('925', 'fixed'), 'rating': ('210', 'input'), 'output': ('178.5', 'live'), 'surplus': ('31.5', 'live')}
 step 2 | Hot primary helium at 925°C passes 121.7MW to a separate secondary loop in the intermediate heat exchanger (IHX), so the reactor coolant never enters the chemical plant. The circulator sends the cooled helium back to the core.
   notes: ['A branch of the hot helium also runs to the power block, which step 12 follows.', 'Input outside its evidence range: IHX approach at 25 K lies outside the evidence range 50 to 82 K, and the cross rules cap the control at 50 K.']
   figs: {'ihx': ('121.7', 'live'), 'he': ('79.2', 'live'), 'e': ('3.96', 'live'), 'app': ('25', 'input')}
 step 3 | The secondary helium leaves the IHX at 900°C, heats the reformer tubes and returns at 466°C, bringing 120.8MWth of process heat that a conventional reformer would raise by burning natural gas. A second circulator on the return leg draws 3.48MWe; the drawing does not show it.
   notes: ["The helium connections on the reformer are drawn schematically. In the engine's tube model the hot helium enters at the process gas outlet end and flows against the process gas.", 'Input outside its evidence range: Helium supply temperature is fixed at 900 C. Highest measured secondary helium temperature is 859 C, HTTR 2004 high-temperature run (IAEA NP-T-4.3 printed p.14).']
   figs: {'sup': ('900', 'fixed'), 'ret': ('466', 'fixed'), 'q': ('120.8', 'live'), 'he': ('54.0', 'live')}
 step 4 | Natural gas at 23.2MMSCFD and process steam enter the reformer, joined by the methane-rich recycle of step 9. In the catalyst tubes methane and steam react to hydrogen and carbon monoxide, taking up the helium's heat. The engine sets the exit at 871°C, INL's equilibrium assumption; with helium at 900°C, the tube model reaches only 782.1°C (precomputed) on the assumed tube.
   notes: []
   figs: {'ng': ('23.2', 'live'), 'out': ('871', 'fixed'), 'tube': ('782.1', 'pre')}
 step 5 | The syngas raises steam as it leaves the reformer and enters the high-temperature shift bed at 335°C. Over the shift catalyst, carbon monoxide and steam form more hydrogen and carbon dioxide, and the beds release 8.1MW of heat.
   notes: ['The drawing shows the two beds, and the coolers between them, as one vessel.']
   figs: {'hts': ('335', 'fixed'), 'lts': ('191', 'fixed'), 'q': ('8.1', 'live')}
 step 6 | The shifted gas is cooled in steps, and 8.4MW of its heat is hot enough to help drive the MEA reboiler of step 10. In the separator at 40°C, 40t/h of water condenses out before the gas goes on to the absorber.
   notes: ['The engine models six exchangers where the drawing shows one cooler.']
   figs: {'ex4': ('8.4', 'live'), 'sep': ('40', 'fixed'), 'cond': ('40', 'live')}
 step 7 | In the packed absorber the gas rises against falling 30wt% aqueous monoethanolamine (MEA) at 26.8bar. The amine takes up 90.0% of the CO₂ in one pass and leaves at the bottom as rich solvent; the hydrogen-rich gas leaves at the top.
   notes: []
   figs: {'pp': ('90.0', 'live'), 'cap': ('1,098', 'live'), 'sol': ('894', 'live'), 'p': ('26.8', 'input')}
 step 8 | The hydrogen-rich gas, with 10,214kg/h of hydrogen, crosses the dashed boundary to pressure swing adsorption (PSA), an external block the engine does not simulate. At an assumed 88.0% recovery it delivers 8,988kg/h of purified hydrogen, 66,925t/y at 85.0% availability.
   notes: ['The two PSA beds are drawn schematically, since the block is external and has no model.']
   figs: {'feed': ('10,214', 'live'), 'prod': ('8,988', 'live'), 'ty': ('66,925', 'live'), 'rec': ('88.0', 'fixed')}
 step 9 | The PSA reject, still holding 1,226kg/h of hydrogen, crosses back into the boundary and is compressed for the cryogenic unit. The cold separation returns hydrogen to the PSA feed and a methane-rich recycle to the reformer. The nitrogen-rich cut is burned and leaves by the stack as the plant's residual 38,309t/y of CO₂.
   notes: ["CO₂ slip into this unit is about ten times INL's, and freeze-out and reverse shift are unquantified, so INL's recycle may not transfer to MEA.", 'The burner of the nitrogen-rich cut is not drawn; the stack stands for it.']
   figs: {'tc': ('472', 'live'), 'rc': ('355', 'live'), 'bc': ('117', 'live'), 'atm': ('38,309', 'live')}
 step 10 | Rich solvent is warmed in the rich/lean exchanger and stripped at 2.0bar by reboiler steam, which drives the CO₂ out; the lean solvent is cooled and pumped back to the absorber. The reboiler needs 51.0MW, met in a fixed order: freed Selexol steam 12.7MW, export steam 16.8MW, syngas heat 8.4MW, and reactor steam 13.1MW last.
   notes: ["No line on the drawing brings heat into the reboiler; the order comes from the engine's energy balance."]
   figs: {'q': ('51.0', 'live'), 'srd': ('3.8', 'input'), 'gap': ('13.1', 'live'), 'ps': ('2.0', 'input')}
 step 11 | The condenser returns water from the stripper overhead as reflux, and the CO₂ is compressed to 138.9bar (precomputed) using 4.25MWe. Then 359,777t/y of CO₂ leaves by ship for storage offshore abroad, which the model treats as a tariff of 60S$/t with no simulation.
   notes: ['How offshore storage abroad counts as abatement is unresolved.']
   figs: {'cap': ('359,777', 'live'), 'e': ('4.25', 'live'), 'p': ('138.9', 'pre'), 'cost': ('21.6', 'live')}
 step 12 | A branch of the hot primary helium carries 59.6MWth to the power block, whose turbine makes 19.8MWe at 39.7% efficiency and sends 13.1MW of steam heat to the reboiler. The helium circulators draw 7.45MWe of the 19.78MWe site demand, and the grid supplies 0.00MWe.
   notes: ["Whether INL's power conversion efficiency is already net of primary circulation is unverified, so this load may be counted twice."]
   figs: {'br': ('59.6', 'live'), 'en': ('19.8', 'live'), 'et': ('19.78', 'live'), 'imp': ('0.00', 'live')}
 step 13 | End to end, the plant makes 66,925t/y of purified hydrogen and sends 359,777t/y of CO₂ to storage. Against the 616,361t/y an unabated reformer would emit on INL's baseline, its residual of 38,309t/y leaves a net abatement of 578,052t/y. Part of that is the CO₂ stored; the rest is never formed, since no natural gas is burned for reformer heat. The abatement target of 250,000t/y is met at these inputs. On Cho's and IEAGHG's baselines the net abatement is 587,436t/y and 563,745t/y.
   notes: ['The plant sits on the proposed Western Island site, a proposal of this study with no official approval.']
   figs: {'h2': ('66,925', 'live'), 'cap': ('359,777', 'live'), 'atm': ('38,309', 'live'), 'ab': ('578,052', 'live')}
===== basis=case_a feasible False

The heat duties fit within the rating and the power branch falls short, so 22.10 MWe is imported. Scenario: Plant basis: Case A, INL reference. ===== tail_gas=burn_on_site feasible True
 step 1 | Scenario: PSA reject fate: Burn on site, H2 held. The drawing shows the headline plant, Case B with the reject recycled, so some drawn lines may carry nothing here. The fission heat passes to helium flowing through the core, which leaves at 925°C. The reactor runs at 128.3MWth of its 210MWth rating.

 step 2 | Scenario: Process heat basis: Ahn 2026 process heat, helium flow following duty. The drawing shows the headline plant, Case B with the reject recycled, so some drawn lines may carry nothing here. Hot primary helium at 925°C passes 144.3MW to a separate secondary loop in the intermediate heat exchanger (IHX), so the reactor coolant never enters the chemical plant. The circulator sends the cooled helium back to the core.

 step 3 | Scenario: Process heat basis: Ahn 2026 process heat, helium flow following duty. The drawing shows the headline plant, Case B with the reject recycled, so some drawn lines may carry nothing here. The secondary helium leaves the IHX at 900°C, heats the reformer tubes and returns at 466°C, bringing 143.4MWth of process heat that a conventional reformer would raise by burning natural gas.  step 4 | Scenario: Process heat basis: Ahn 2026 process heat, helium flow following duty. The drawing shows the headline plant, Case B with the reject recycled, so some drawn lines may carry nothing here. Natural gas at 23.2MMSCFD and process steam enter the reformer, joined by the methane-rich recycle of step 9. In the catalyst tubes methane and steam react to hydrogen and carbon monoxide, taking up the helium's heat. The engine sets the exit at 871°C, INL's equilibrium assumption; with helium at 900°C, the tube model reaches only 782.1°C (precomputed) on the assumed tube.

 step 5 | Scenario: Process heat basis: Ahn 2026 process heat, helium flow following duty. The drawing shows the headline plant, Case B with the reject recycled, so some drawn lines may carry nothing here. The syngas raises steam as it leaves the reformer and enters the high-temperature shift bed at 335°C. Over the shift catalyst, carbon monoxide and steam form more hydrogen and carbon dioxide, and the beds release 8.1MW of heat.
   notes: ['The drawing shows the two beds, and the coolers between them, as one vessel.']

 step 6 | Scenario: Process heat basis: Ahn 2026 process heat, helium flow following duty. The shifted gas is cooled in steps, and 8.4MW of its heat is hot enough to help drive the MEA reboiler of step 10. In the separator at 40°C, 40t/h of water condenses out before the gas goes on to the absorber.

 step 7 | Scenario: Process heat basis: Ahn 2026 process heat, helium flow following duty. The drawing shows the headline plant, Case B with the reject recycled, so some drawn lines may carry nothing here. In the packed absorber the gas rises against falling 30wt% aqueous monoethanolamine (MEA) at 26.8bar. The amine takes up 90.0% of the CO₂ in one pass and leaves at the bottom as rich solvent; the hydrogen-rich gas leaves at the top.

 step 8 | Scenario: Process heat basis: Ahn 2026 process heat, helium flow following duty. The drawing shows the headline plant, Case B with the reject recycled, so some drawn lines may carry nothing here. The hydrogen-rich gas, with 10,214kg/h of hydrogen, crosses the dashed boundary to pressure swing adsorption (PSA), an external block the engine does not simulate. At an assumed 88.0% recovery it delivers 8,988kg/h of purified hydrogen, 66,925t/y at 85.0% availability.

 step 9 | Scenario: Process heat basis: Ahn 2026 process heat, helium flow following duty. The drawing shows the headline plant, Case B with the reject recycled, so some drawn lines may carry nothing here. The PSA reject, still holding 1,226kg/h of hydrogen, crosses back into the boundary and is compressed for the cryogenic unit. The cold separation returns hydrogen to the PSA feed and a methane-rich recycle to the reformer. The nitrogen-rich cut is burned and leaves by the stack as the plant's residual 38,309t/y of CO₂.
   notes: ["CO₂ slip into this unit is about ten times INL's, and freeze-out and reverse shift are unquantified, so INL's recycle may not transfer to MEA.", 'The burner of the nitrogen-rich cut is not drawn; the stack stands for it.']

 step 10 | Scenario: Process heat basis: Ahn 2026 process heat, helium flow following duty. The drawing shows the headline plant, Case B with the reject recycled, so some drawn lines may carry nothing here. Rich solvent is warmed in the rich/lean exchanger and stripped at 2.0bar by reboiler steam, which drives the CO₂ out; the lean solvent is cooled and pumped back to the absorber. The reboiler needs 51.0MW, met in a fixed order: freed Selexol steam 12.7MW, export steam 16.8MW, syngas heat 8.4MW, and reactor steam 13.1MW last.
   notes: ["No line on the drawing brings heat into the reboiler; the order comes from the engine's energy balance."]
   figs: {'q': ('51.0', 'live'), 'srd': ('3.8', 'input'), 'gap': ('13.1', 'live'), 'ps': ('2.0', 'input')}
 step 11 | Scenario: Process heat basis: Ahn 2026 process heat, helium flow following duty. The drawing shows the headline plant, Case B with the reject recycled, so some drawn lines may carry nothing here. The condenser returns water from the stripper overhead as reflux, and the CO₂ is compressed to 138.9bar (precomputed) using 4.25MWe. Then 359,777t/y of CO₂ leaves by ship for storage offshore abroad, which the model treats as a tariff of 60S$/t with no simulation.
   notes: ['How offshore storage abroad counts as abatement is unresolved.']
   figs: {'cap': ('359,777', 'live'), 'e': ('4.25', 'live'), 'p': ('138.9', 'pre'), 'cost': ('21.6', 'live')}
 step 12 | Scenario: Process heat basis: Ahn 2026 process heat, helium flow following duty. The drawing shows the headline plant, Case B with the reject recycled, so some drawn lines may carry nothing here. A branch of the hot primary helium carries 62.6MWth to the power block, whose turbine makes 21.0MWe at 39.7% efficiency and sends 13.1MW of steam heat to the reboiler. The helium circulators draw 8.65MWe of the 20.98MWe site demand, and the grid supplies 0.00MWe.
   notes: ["Whether INL's power conversion efficiency is already net of primary circulation is unverified, so this load may be counted twice."]
   figs: {'br': ('62.6', 'live'), 'en': ('21.0', 'live'), 'et': ('20.98', 'live'), 'imp': ('0.00', 'live')}
 step 13 | Scenario: Process heat basis: Ahn 2026 process heat, helium flow following duty. The drawing shows the headline plant, Case B with the reject recycled, so some drawn lines may carry nothing here. End to end, the plant makes 66,925t/y of purified hydrogen and sends 359,777t/y of CO₂ to storage. Against the 616,361t/y an unabated reformer would emit on INL's baseline, its residual of 38,309t/y leaves a net abatement of 578,052t/y. Part of that is the CO₂ stored; the rest is never formed, since no natural gas is burned for reformer heat. The abatement target of 250,000t/y is met at these inputs. On Cho's and IEAGHG's baselines the net abatement is 587,436t/y and 563,745t/y.
   notes: ['The plant sits on the proposed Western Island site, a proposal of this study with no official approval.']
   figs: {'h2': ('66,925', 'live'), 'cap': ('359,777', 'live'), 'atm': ('38,309', 'live'), 'ab': ('578,052', 'live')}

   figs: {'ex4': ('8.4', 'live'), 'sep': ('40', 'fixed'), 'cond': ('40', 'live')}
 step 7 | In the packed absorber the gas rises against falling 30wt% aqueous monoethanolamine (MEA) at 26.8bar. The amine takes up 90.0% of the CO₂ in one pass and leaves at the bottom as rich solvent; the hydrogen-rich gas leaves at the top.
   notes: []
   figs: {'pp': ('90.0', 'live'), 'cap': ('1,098', 'live'), 'sol': ('894', 'live'), 'p': ('26.8', 'input')}
 step 8 | The hydrogen-rich gas, with 10,214kg/h of hydrogen, crosses the dashed boundary to pressure swing adsorption (PSA), an external block the engine does not simulate. At an assumed 88.0% recovery it delivers 8,988kg/h of purified hydrogen, 66,925t/y at 85.0% availability.
   notes: ['The two PSA beds are drawn schematically, since the block is external and has no model.']
   figs: {'feed': ('10,214', 'live'), 'prod': ('8,988', 'live'), 'ty': ('66,925', 'live'), 'rec': ('88.0', 'fixed')}
 step 9 | The PSA reject, still holding 1,226kg/h of hydrogen, crosses back into the boundary and is compressed for the cryogenic unit. The cold separation returns hydrogen to the PSA feed and a methane-rich recycle to the reformer. The nitrogen-rich cut is burned and leaves by the stack as the plant's residual 38,309t/y of CO₂.
   notes: ["CO₂ slip into this unit is about ten times INL's, and freeze-out and reverse shift are unquantified, so INL's recycle may not transfer to MEA.", 'The burner of the nitrogen-rich cut is not drawn; the stack stands for it.']
   figs: {'tc': ('472', 'live'), 'rc': ('355', 'live'), 'bc': ('117', 'live'), 'atm': ('38,309', 'live')}
 step 10 | Rich solvent is warmed in the rich/lean exchanger and stripped at 2.0bar by reboiler steam, which drives the CO₂ out; the lean solvent is cooled and pumped back to the absorber. The reboiler needs 51.0MW, met in a fixed order: freed Selexol steam 12.7MW, export steam 16.8MW, syngas heat 8.4MW, and reactor steam 13.1MW last.
   notes: ["No line on the drawing brings heat into the reboiler; the order comes from the engine's energy balance."]
   figs: {'q': ('51.0', 'live'), 'srd': ('3.8', 'input'), 'gap': ('13.1', 'live'), 'ps': ('2.0', 'input')}
 step 11 | The condenser returns water from the stripper overhead as reflux, and the CO₂ is compressed to 138.9bar (precomputed) using 4.25MWe. Then 359,777t/y of CO₂ leaves by ship for storage offshore abroad, which the model treats as a tariff of 60S$/t with no simulation.
   notes: ['How offshore storage abroad counts as abatement is unresolved.']
   figs: {'cap': ('359,777', 'live'), 'e': ('4.25', 'live'), 'p': ('138.9', 'pre'), 'cost': ('21.6', 'live')}
 step 12 | A branch of the hot primary helium carries 60.2MWth to the power block, whose turbine makes 20.0MWe at 39.7% efficiency and sends 13.1MW of steam heat to the reboiler. The helium circulators draw 7.70MWe of the 20.03MWe site demand, and the grid supplies 0.00MWe.
   notes: ["Whether INL's power conversion efficiency is already net of primary circulation is unverified, so this load may be counted twice."]
   figs: {'br': ('60.2', 'live'), 'en': ('20.0', 'live'), 'et': ('20.03', 'live'), 'imp': ('0.00', 'live')}
 step 13 | End to end, the plant makes 66,925t/y of purified hydrogen and sends 359,777t/y of CO₂ to storage. Against the 616,361t/y an unabated reformer would emit on INL's baseline, its residual of 38,309t/y leaves a net abatement of 578,052t/y. Part of that is the CO₂ stored; the rest is never formed, since no natural gas is burned for reformer heat. The abatement target of 250,000t/y is met at these inputs. On Cho's and IEAGHG's baselines the net abatement is 587,436t/y and 563,745t/y.
   notes: ['The plant sits on the proposed Western Island site, a proposal of this study with no official approval.']
   figs: {'h2': ('66,925', 'live'), 'cap': ('359,777', 'live'), 'atm': ('38,309', 'live'), 'ab': ('578,052', 'live')}

 step 1 | Inside the core, uranium nuclei in the TRISO fuel particles split when they absorb a neutron, and the graphite slows the new neutrons so the chain reaction carries on.  step 3 | The secondary helium leaves the IHX at 900°C, heats the reformer tubes and returns at 466°C, bringing 120.8MWth of process heat that a conventional reformer would raise by burning natural gas. A second circulator on the return leg draws 3.48MWe; the drawing does not show it.
   notes: ["The helium connections on the reformer are drawn schematically.    figs: {'sup': ('900', 'fixed'), 'ret': ('466', 'fixed'), 'q': ('120.8', 'live'), 'he': ('54.0', 'live')}
 step 4 | Natural gas at 23.2MMSCFD and process steam enter the reformer, joined by the methane-rich recycle of step 9. In the catalyst tubes methane and steam react to hydrogen and carbon monoxide, taking up the helium's heat. The engine sets the exit at 871°C, INL's equilibrium assumption; with helium at 900°C, the tube model reaches only 782.1°C (precomputed) on the assumed tube.
   notes: []
   figs: {'ng': ('23.2', 'live'), 'out': ('871', 'fixed'), 'tube': ('782.1', 'pre')}
 step 5 | The syngas raises steam as it leaves the reformer and enters the high-temperature shift bed at 335°C. Over the shift catalyst, carbon monoxide and steam form more hydrogen and carbon dioxide, and the beds release 8.1MW of heat.
   notes: ['The drawing shows the two beds, and the coolers between them, as one vessel.']
   figs: {'hts': ('335', 'fixed'), 'lts': ('191', 'fixed'), 'q': ('8.1', 'live')}
 step 6 | The shifted gas is cooled in steps, and 8.4MW of its heat is hot enough to help drive the MEA reboiler of step 10. In the separator at 40°C, 40t/h of water condenses out before the gas goes on to the absorber.
   notes: ['The engine models six exchangers where the drawing shows one cooler.']
   figs: {'ex4': ('8.4', 'live'), 'sep': ('40', 'fixed'), 'cond': ('40', 'live')}
 step 7 | In the packed absorber the gas rises against falling 30wt% aqueous monoethanolamine (MEA) at 26.8bar. The amine takes up 90.0% of the CO₂ in one pass and leaves at the bottom as rich solvent; the hydrogen-rich gas leaves at the top.
   notes: []
   figs: {'pp': ('90.0', 'live'), 'cap': ('1,098', 'live'), 'sol': ('1,118', 'live'), 'p': ('26.8', 'input')}
 step 8 | The hydrogen-rich gas, with 10,214kg/h of hydrogen, crosses the dashed boundary to pressure swing adsorption (PSA), an external block the engine does not simulate. At an assumed 88.0% recovery it delivers 8,988kg/h of purified hydrogen, 66,925t/y at 85.0% availability.
   notes: ['The two PSA beds are drawn schematically, since the block is external and has no model.']
   figs: {'feed': ('10,214', 'live'), 'prod': ('8,988', 'live'), 'ty': ('66,925', 'live'), 'rec': ('88.0', 'fixed')}
 step 9 | The PSA reject, still holding 1,226kg/h of hydrogen, crosses back into the boundary and is compressed for the cryogenic unit. The cold separation returns hydrogen to the PSA feed and a methane-rich recycle to the reformer. The nitrogen-rich cut is burned and leaves by the stack as the plant's residual 38,309t/y of CO₂.
   notes: ["CO₂ slip into this unit is about ten times INL's, and freeze-out and reverse shift are unquantified, so INL's recycle may not transfer to MEA.", 'The burner of the nitrogen-rich cut is not drawn; the stack stands for it.']
   figs: {'tc': ('472', 'live'), 'rc': ('355', 'live'), 'bc': ('117', 'live'), 'atm': ('38,309', 'live')}
 step 10 | Rich solvent is warmed in the rich/lean exchanger and stripped at 2.0bar by reboiler steam, which drives the CO₂ out; the lean solvent is cooled and pumped back to the absorber. The reboiler needs 51.0MW, met in a fixed order: freed Selexol steam 12.7MW, export steam 16.8MW, syngas heat 8.4MW, and reactor steam 13.1MW last.
   notes: ["No line on the drawing brings heat into the reboiler; the order comes from the engine's energy balance."]
   figs: {'q': ('51.0', 'live'), 'srd': ('3.8', 'input'), 'gap': ('13.1', 'live'), 'ps': ('2.0', 'input')}
 step 11 | The condenser returns water from the stripper overhead as reflux, and the CO₂ is compressed to 138.9bar (precomputed) using 4.25MWe. Then 359,777t/y of CO₂ leaves by ship for storage offshore abroad, which the model treats as a tariff of 60S$/t with no simulation.
   notes: ['How offshore storage abroad counts as abatement is unresolved.']
   figs: {'cap': ('359,777', 'live'), 'e': ('4.25', 'live'), 'p': ('138.9', 'pre'), 'cost': ('21.6', 'live')}
 step 12 | A branch of the hot primary helium carries 60.2MWth to the power block, whose turbine makes 20.0MWe at 39.7% efficiency and sends 13.1MW of steam heat to the reboiler. The helium circulators draw 7.46MWe of the 20.01MWe site demand, and the grid supplies 0.00MWe.
   notes: ["Whether INL's power conversion efficiency is already net of primary circulation is unverified, so this load may be counted twice."]
   figs: {'br': ('60.2', 'live'), 'en': ('20.0', 'live'), 'et': ('20.01', 'live'), 'imp': ('0.00', 'live')}
 step 13 | End to end, the plant makes 66,925t/y of purified hydrogen and sends 359,777t/y of CO₂ to storage. Against the 616,361t/y an unabated reformer would emit on INL's baseline, its residual of 38,309t/y leaves a net abatement of 578,052t/y. Part of that is the CO₂ stored; the rest is never formed, since no natural gas is burned for reformer heat. The abatement target of 250,000t/y is met at these inputs. On Cho's and IEAGHG's baselines the net abatement is 587,436t/y and 563,745t/y.
   notes: ['The plant sits on the proposed Western Island site, a proposal of this study with no official approval.']
   figs: {'h2': ('66,925', 'live'), 'cap': ('359,777', 'live'), 'atm': ('38,309', 'live'), 'ab': ('578,052', 'live')}

 step 13 | End to end, the plant makes 66,925t/y of purified hydrogen and sends 357,173t/y of CO₂ to storage. ===== srd=4.2 feasible True
 step 1 | Inside the core, uranium nuclei in the TRISO fuel particles split when they absorb a neutron, and the graphite slows the new neutrons so the chain reaction carries on. The fission heat passes to helium flowing through the core, which leaves at 925°C. The reactor runs at 182.9MWth of its 210MWth rating.
   notes: ['The design basis is a generic 925°C HTGR. No commercial reactor runs at this outlet, and Xe-100 is only a labelled analogue with a much lower outlet temperature.']
   figs: {'rot': ('925', 'fixed'), 'rating': ('210', 'input'), 'output': ('182.9', 'live'), 'surplus': ('27.1', 'live')}
 step 2 | Hot primary helium at 925°C passes 121.7MW to a separate secondary loop in the intermediate heat exchanger (IHX), so the reactor coolant never enters the chemical plant. The circulator sends the cooled helium back to the core.
   notes: ['A branch of the hot helium also runs to the power block, which step 12 follows.', 'Input outside its evidence range: IHX approach at 25 K lies outside the evidence range 50 to 82 K, and the cross rules cap the control at 50 K.']
   figs: {'ihx': ('121.7', 'live'), 'he': ('81.1', 'live'), 'e': ('4.06', 'live'), 'app': ('25', 'input')}
 step 3 | The secondary helium leaves the IHX at 900°C, heats the reformer tubes and returns at 466°C, bringing 120.8MWth of process heat that a conventional reformer would raise by burning natural gas. A second circulator on the return leg draws 3.48MWe; the drawing does not show it.
   notes: ["The helium connections on the reformer are drawn schematically.    notes: ["No line on the drawing brings heat into the reboiler; the order comes from the engine's energy balance."]
   figs: {'q': ('56.4', 'live'), 'srd': ('4.2', 'input'), 'gap': ('18.5', 'live'), 'ps': ('2.0', 'input')}

   notes: ["CO₂ slip into this unit is about ten times INL's, and freeze-out and reverse shift are unquantified, so INL's recycle may not transfer to MEA.", 'The burner of the nitrogen-rich cut is not drawn; the stack stands for it.']
   figs: {'tc': ('472', 'live'), 'rc': ('355', 'live'), 'bc': ('117', 'live'), 'atm': ('40,562', 'live')}
 step 10 | Rich solvent is warmed in the rich/lean exchanger and stripped at 2.0bar by reboiler steam, which drives the CO₂ out; the lean solvent is cooled and pumped back to the absorber. The reboiler needs 51.0MW, met in a fixed order: freed Selexol steam 12.7MW, export steam 16.8MW, syngas heat 8.4MW, and reactor steam 13.1MW last.
   notes: ["No line on the drawing brings heat into the reboiler; the order comes from the engine's energy balance."]
   figs: {'q': ('51.0', 'live'), 'srd': ('3.8', 'input'), 'gap': ('13.1', 'live'), 'ps': ('2.0', 'input')}
 step 11 | The condenser returns water from the stripper overhead as reflux, and the CO₂ is compressed to 138.9bar (precomputed) using 4.25MWe. Then 380,941t/y of CO₂ leaves by ship for storage offshore abroad, which the model treats as a tariff of 60S$/t with no simulation.
   notes: ['How offshore storage abroad counts as abatement is unresolved.']
   figs: {'cap': ('380,941', 'live'), 'e': ('4.25', 'live'), 'p': ('138.9', 'pre'), 'cost': ('22.9', 'live')}
 step 12 | A branch of the hot primary helium carries 59.6MWth to the power block, whose turbine makes 19.8MWe at 39.7% efficiency and sends 13.1MW of steam heat to the reboiler. The helium circulators draw 7.45MWe of the 19.78MWe site demand, and the grid supplies 0.00MWe.
   notes: ["Whether INL's power conversion efficiency is already net of primary circulation is unverified, so this load may be counted twice."]
   figs: {'br': ('59.6', 'live'), 'en': ('19.8', 'live'), 'et': ('19.78', 'live'), 'imp': ('0.00', 'live')}
 step 13 | End to end, the plant makes 70,861t/y of purified hydrogen and sends 380,941t/y of CO₂ to storage. Against the 652,617t/y an unabated reformer would emit on INL's baseline, its residual of 40,562t/y leaves a net abatement of 612,055t/y. Part of that is the CO₂ stored; the rest is never formed, since no natural gas is burned for reformer heat. The abatement target of 250,000t/y is met at these inputs. On Cho's and IEAGHG's baselines the net abatement is 621,992t/y and 596,906t/y.
   notes: ['The plant sits on the proposed Western Island site, a proposal of this study with no official approval.']
   figs: {'h2': ('70,861', 'live'), 'cap': ('380,941', 'live'), 'atm': ('40,562', 'live'), 'ab': ('612,055', 'live')}
===== availability=0.65 feasible True
 step 1 | Inside the core, uranium nuclei in the TRISO fuel particles split when they absorb a neutron, and the graphite slows the new neutrons so the chain reaction carries on. The fission heat passes to helium flowing through the core, which leaves at 925°C. The reactor runs at 178.5MWth of its 210MWth rating.
   notes: ['The design basis is a generic 925°C HTGR. No commercial reactor runs at this outlet, and Xe-100 is only a labelled analogue with a much lower outlet temperature.']
   figs: {'rot': ('925', 'fixed'), 'rating': ('210', 'input'), 'output': ('178.5', 'live'), 'surplus': ('31.5', 'live')}
 step 2 | Hot primary helium at 925°C passes 121.7MW to a separate secondary loop in the intermediate heat exchanger (IHX), so the reactor coolant never enters the chemical plant. The circulator sends the cooled helium back to the core.
   notes: ['A branch of the hot helium also runs to the power block, which step 12 follows.', 'Input outside its evidence range: IHX approach at 25 K lies outside the evidence range 50 to 82 K, and the cross rules cap the control at 50 K.']
   figs: {'ihx': ('121.7', 'live'), 'he': ('79.2', 'live'), 'e': ('3.96', 'live'), 'app': ('25', 'input')}
 step 3 | The secondary helium leaves the IHX at 900°C, heats the reformer tubes and returns at 466°C, bringing 120.8MWth of process heat that a conventional reformer would raise by burning natural gas. A second circulator on the return leg draws 3.48MWe; the drawing does not show it.
   notes: ["The helium connections on the reformer are drawn schematically. In the engine's tube model the hot helium enters at the process gas outlet end and flows against the process gas.", 'Input outside its evidence range: Helium supply temperature is fixed at 900 C. Highest measured secondary helium temperature is 859 C, HTTR 2004 high-temperature run (IAEA NP-T-4.3 printed p.14).']
   figs: {'sup': ('900', 'fixed'), 'ret': ('466', 'fixed'), 'q': ('120.8', 'live'), 'he': ('54.0', 'live')}
 step 4 | Natural gas at 23.2MMSCFD and process steam enter the reformer, joined by the methane-rich recycle of step 9. In the catalyst tubes methane and steam react to hydrogen and carbon monoxide, taking up the helium's heat. The engine sets the exit at 871°C, INL's equilibrium assumption; with helium at 900°C, the tube model reaches only 782.1°C (precomputed) on the assumed tube.
   notes: []
   figs: {'ng': ('23.2', 'live'), 'out': ('871', 'fixed'), 'tube': ('782.1', 'pre')}

   notes: ['The drawing shows the two beds, and the coolers between them, as one vessel.']
   figs: {'hts': ('335', 'fixed'), 'lts': ('191', 'fixed'), 'q': ('8.1', 'live')}
 step 6 | The shifted gas is cooled in steps, and 8.4MW of its heat is hot enough to help drive the MEA reboiler of step 10. In the separator at 40°C, 40t/h of water condenses out before the gas goes on to the absorber.

 step 5 | The syngas raises steam as it leaves the reformer and enters the high-temperature shift bed at 335°C. Over the shift catalyst, carbon monoxide and steam form more hydrogen and carbon dioxide, and the beds release 8.1MW of heat.
   notes: ['The drawing shows the two beds, and the coolers between them, as one vessel.']
   figs: {'hts': ('335', 'fixed'), 'lts': ('191', 'fixed'), 'q': ('8.1', 'live')}
 step 6 | The shifted gas is cooled in steps, and 8.4MW of its heat is hot enough to help drive the MEA reboiler of step 10. In the separator at 40°C, 40t/h of water condenses out before the gas goes on to the absorber.
   notes: ['The engine models six exchangers where the drawing shows one cooler.']
   figs: {'ex4': ('8.4', 'live'), 'sep': ('40', 'fixed'), 'cond': ('40', 'live')}
 step 7 | In the packed absorber the gas rises against falling 30wt% aqueous monoethanolamine (MEA) at 26.8bar. The amine takes up 90.0% of the CO₂ in one pass and leaves at the bottom as rich solvent; the hydrogen-rich gas leaves at the top.
   notes: []
   figs: {'pp': ('90.0', 'live'), 'cap': ('1,098', 'live'), 'sol': ('894', 'live'), 'p': ('26.8', 'input')}
 step 8 | The hydrogen-rich gas, with 10,214kg/h of hydrogen, crosses the dashed boundary to pressure swing adsorption (PSA), an external block the engine does not simulate. At an assumed 88.0% recovery it delivers 8,988kg/h of purified hydrogen, 66,925t/y at 85.0% availability.
   notes: ['The two PSA beds are drawn schematically, since the block is external and has no model.']
   figs: {'feed': ('10,214', 'live'), 'prod': ('8,988', 'live'), 'ty': ('66,925', 'live'), 'rec': ('88.0', 'fixed')}
 step 9 | The PSA reject, still holding 1,226kg/h of hydrogen, crosses back into the boundary and is compressed for the cryogenic unit. The cold separation returns hydrogen to the PSA feed and a methane-rich recycle to the reformer. The nitrogen-rich cut is burned and leaves by the stack as the plant's residual 38,309t/y of CO₂.
   notes: ["CO₂ slip into this unit is about ten times INL's, and freeze-out and reverse shift are unquantified, so INL's recycle may not transfer to MEA.", 'The burner of the nitrogen-rich cut is not drawn; the stack stands for it.']
   figs: {'tc': ('472', 'live'), 'rc': ('355', 'live'), 'bc': ('117', 'live'), 'atm': ('38,309', 'live')}
 step 10 | Rich solvent is warmed in the rich/lean exchanger and stripped at 2.0bar by reboiler steam, which drives the CO₂ out; the lean solvent is cooled and pumped back to the absorber. The reboiler needs 51.0MW, met in a fixed order: freed Selexol steam 12.7MW, export steam 16.8MW, syngas heat 8.4MW, and reactor steam 13.1MW last.
   notes: ["No line on the drawing brings heat into the reboiler; the order comes from the engine's energy balance."]
   figs: {'q': ('51.0', 'live'), 'srd': ('3.8', 'input'), 'gap': ('13.1', 'live'), 'ps': ('2.0', 'input')}
 step 11 | The condenser returns water from the stripper overhead as reflux, and the CO₂ is compressed to 138.9bar (precomputed) using 4.25MWe. Then 359,777t/y of CO₂ leaves by ship for storage offshore abroad, which the model treats as a tariff of 60S$/t with no simulation.
   notes: ['How offshore storage abroad counts as abatement is unresolved.']
   figs: {'cap': ('359,777', 'live'), 'e': ('4.25', 'live'), 'p': ('138.9', 'pre'), 'cost': ('21.6', 'live')}
 step 12 | A branch of the hot primary helium carries 59.6MWth to the power block, whose turbine makes 19.8MWe at 39.7% efficiency and sends 13.1MW of steam heat to the reboiler. The helium circulators draw 7.45MWe of the 19.78MWe site demand, and the grid supplies 0.00MWe.
   notes: ["Whether INL's power conversion efficiency is already net of primary circulation is unverified, so this load may be counted twice."]
   figs: {'br': ('59.6', 'live'), 'en': ('19.8', 'live'), 'et': ('19.78', 'live'), 'imp': ('0.00', 'live')}
 step 13 | End to end, the plant makes 66,925t/y of purified hydrogen and sends 359,777t/y of CO₂ to storage. Against the 616,361t/y an unabated reformer would emit on INL's baseline, its residual of 38,309t/y leaves a net abatement of 578,052t/y. Part of that is the CO₂ stored; the rest is never formed, since no natural gas is burned for reformer heat. The abatement target of 250,000t/y is met at these inputs. On Cho's and IEAGHG's baselines the net abatement is 587,436t/y and 563,745t/y.
   notes: ['The plant sits on the proposed Western Island site, a proposal of this study with no official approval.']
   figs: {'h2': ('66,925', 'live'), 'cap': ('359,777', 'live'), 'atm': ('38,309', 'live'), 'ab': ('578,052', 'live')}
===== life_y=40 feasible True
 step 1 | Inside the core, uranium nuclei in the TRISO fuel particles split when they absorb a neutron, and the graphite slows the new neutrons so the chain reaction carries on. The fission heat passes to helium flowing through the core, which leaves at 925°C. The reactor runs at 178.5MWth of its 210MWth rating.
   notes: ['The design basis is a generic 925°C HTGR. No commercial reactor runs at this outlet, and Xe-100 is only a labelled analogue with a much lower outlet temperature.']
   figs: {'rot': ('925', 'fixed'), 'rating': ('210', 'input'), 'output': ('178.5', 'live'), 'surplus': ('31.5', 'live')}
 step 2 | Hot primary helium at 925°C passes 121.7MW to a separate secondary loop in the intermediate heat exchanger (IHX), so the reactor coolant never enters the chemical plant. The circulator sends the cooled helium back to the core.
   notes: ['A branch of the hot helium also runs to the power block, which step 12 follows.', 'Input outside its evidence range: IHX approach at 25 K lies outside the evidence range 50 to 82 K, and the cross rules cap the control at 50 K.']
   figs: {'ihx': ('121.7', 'live'), 'he': ('79.2', 'live'), 'e': ('3.96', 'live'), 'app': ('25', 'input')}

 step 2 | Hot primary helium at 925°C passes 121.7MW to a separate secondary loop in the intermediate heat exchanger (IHX), so the reactor coolant never enters the chemical plant. The circulator sends the cooled helium back to the core.
   notes: ['A branch of the hot helium also runs to the power block, which step 12 follows.', 'Input outside its evidence range: IHX approach at 25 K lies outside the evidence range 50 to 82 K, and the cross rules cap the control at 50 K.']
   figs: {'ihx': ('121.7', 'live'), 'he': ('79.2', 'live'), 'e': ('3.96', 'live'), 'app': ('25', 'input')}
 step 3 | The secondary helium leaves the IHX at 900°C, heats the reformer tubes and returns at 466°C, bringing 120.8MWth of process heat that a conventional reformer would raise by burning natural gas. A second circulator on the return leg draws 3.48MWe; the drawing does not show it.
   notes: ["The helium connections on the reformer are drawn schematically. In the engine's tube model the hot helium enters at the process gas outlet end and flows against the process gas.", 'Input outside its evidence range: Helium supply temperature is fixed at 900 C. Highest measured secondary helium temperature is 859 C, HTTR 2004 high-temperature run (IAEA NP-T-4.3 printed p.14).']
   figs: {'sup': ('900', 'fixed'), 'ret': ('466', 'fixed'), 'q': ('120.8', 'live'), 'he': ('54.0', 'live')}
 step 4 | Natural gas at 23.2MMSCFD and process steam enter the reformer, joined by the methane-rich recycle of step 9. In the catalyst tubes methane and steam react to hydrogen and carbon monoxide, taking up the helium's heat. The engine sets the exit at 871°C, INL's equilibrium assumption; with helium at 900°C, the tube model reaches only 782.1°C (precomputed) on the assumed tube.
   notes: []
   figs: {'ng': ('23.2', 'live'), 'out': ('871', 'fixed'), 'tube': ('782.1', 'pre')}
 step 5 | The syngas raises steam as it leaves the reformer and enters the high-temperature shift bed at 335°C. Over the shift catalyst, carbon monoxide and steam form more hydrogen and carbon dioxide, and the beds release 8.1MW of heat.
   notes: ['The drawing shows the two beds, and the coolers between them, as one vessel.']
   figs: {'hts': ('335', 'fixed'), 'lts': ('191', 'fixed'), 'q': ('8.1', 'live')}
 step 6 | The shifted gas is cooled in steps, and 8.4MW of its heat is hot enough to help drive the MEA reboiler of step 10. In the separator at 40°C, 40t/h of water condenses out before the gas goes on to the absorber.
   notes: ['The engine models six exchangers where the drawing shows one cooler.']
   figs: {'ex4': ('8.4', 'live'), 'sep': ('40', 'fixed'), 'cond': ('40', 'live')}
 step 7 | In the packed absorber the gas rises against falling 30wt% aqueous monoethanolamine (MEA) at 26.8bar. The amine takes up 90.0% of the CO₂ in one pass and leaves at the bottom as rich solvent; the hydrogen-rich gas leaves at the top.
   notes: []
   figs: {'pp': ('90.0', 'live'), 'cap': ('1,098', 'live'), 'sol': ('894', 'live'), 'p': ('26.8', 'input')}
 step 8 | The hydrogen-rich gas, with 10,214kg/h of hydrogen, crosses the dashed boundary to pressure swing adsorption (PSA), an external block the engine does not simulate. At an assumed 88.0% recovery it delivers 8,988kg/h of purified hydrogen, 66,925t/y at 85.0% availability.
   notes: ['The two PSA beds are drawn schematically, since the block is external and has no model.']
   figs: {'feed': ('10,214', 'live'), 'prod': ('8,988', 'live'), 'ty': ('66,925', 'live'), 'rec': ('88.0', 'fixed')}
 step 9 | The PSA reject, still holding 1,226kg/h of hydrogen, crosses back into the boundary and is compressed for the cryogenic unit. The cold separation returns hydrogen to the PSA feed and a methane-rich recycle to the reformer. The nitrogen-rich cut is burned and leaves by the stack as the plant's residual 38,309t/y of CO₂.
   notes: ["CO₂ slip into this unit is about ten times INL's, and freeze-out and reverse shift are unquantified, so INL's recycle may not transfer to MEA.", 'The burner of the nitrogen-rich cut is not drawn; the stack stands for it.']
   figs: {'tc': ('472', 'live'), 'rc': ('355', 'live'), 'bc': ('117', 'live'), 'atm': ('38,309', 'live')}
 step 10 | Rich solvent is warmed in the rich/lean exchanger and stripped at 2.0bar by reboiler steam, which drives the CO₂ out; the lean solvent is cooled and pumped back to the absorber. The reboiler needs 51.0MW, met in a fixed order: freed Selexol steam 12.7MW, export steam 16.8MW, syngas heat 8.4MW, and reactor steam 13.1MW last.
   notes: ["No line on the drawing brings heat into the reboiler; the order comes from the engine's energy balance."]
   figs: {'q': ('51.0', 'live'), 'srd': ('3.8', 'input'), 'gap': ('13.1', 'live'), 'ps': ('2.0', 'input')}
 step 11 | The condenser returns water from the stripper overhead as reflux, and the CO₂ is compressed to 138.9bar (precomputed) using 4.25MWe. Then 359,777t/y of CO₂ leaves by ship for storage offshore abroad, which the model treats as a tariff of 60S$/t with no simulation.
   notes: ['How offshore storage abroad counts as abatement is unresolved.']
   figs: {'cap': ('359,777', 'live'), 'e': ('4.25', 'live'), 'p': ('138.9', 'pre'), 'cost': ('21.6', 'live')}
 step 12 | A branch of the hot primary helium carries 59.6MWth to the power block, whose turbine makes 19.8MWe at 39.7% efficiency and sends 13.1MW of steam heat to the reboiler. The helium circulators draw 7.45MWe of the 19.78MWe site demand, and the grid supplies 0.00MWe.
   notes: ["Whether INL's power conversion efficiency is already net of primary circulation is unverified, so this load may be counted twice."]
   figs: {'br': ('59.6', 'live'), 'en': ('19.8', 'live'), 'et': ('19.78', 'live'), 'imp': ('0.00', 'live')}
 step 13 | End to end, the plant makes 66,925t/y of purified hydrogen and sends 359,777t/y of CO₂ to storage. Against the 616,361t/y an unabated reformer would emit on INL's baseline, its residual of 38,309t/y leaves a net abatement of 578,052t/y. Part of that is the CO₂ stored; the rest is never formed, since no natural gas is burned for reformer heat. The abatement target of 250,000t/y is met at these inputs. On Cho's and IEAGHG's baselines the net abatement is 587,436t/y and 563,745t/y.
   notes: ['The plant sits on the proposed Western Island site, a proposal of this study with no official approval.']
   figs: {'h2': ('66,925', 'live'), 'cap': ('359,777', 'live'), 'atm': ('38,309', 'live'), 'ab': ('578,052', 'live')}

 step 12 | A branch of the hot primary helium carries 59.6MWth to the power block, whose turbine makes 19.8MWe at 39.7% efficiency and sends 13.1MW of steam heat to the reboiler. The helium circulators draw 7.45MWe of the 19.78MWe site demand, and the grid supplies 0.00MWe.
   notes: ["Whether INL's power conversion efficiency is already net of primary circulation is unverified, so this load may be counted twice."]
   figs: {'br': ('59.6', 'live'), 'en': ('19.8', 'live'), 'et': ('19.78', 'live'), 'imp': ('0.00', 'live')}
 step 13 | End to end, the plant makes 66,925t/y of purified hydrogen and sends 359,777t/y of CO₂ to storage. Against the 616,361t/y an unabated reformer would emit on INL's baseline, its residual of 38,309t/y leaves a net abatement of 578,052t/y. Part of that is the CO₂ stored; the rest is never formed, since no natural gas is burned for reformer heat. The abatement target of 250,000t/y is met at these inputs. On Cho's and IEAGHG's baselines the net abatement is 587,436t/y and 563,745t/y.

===== gas_s_gj=20 feasible True
 step 1 | Inside the core, uranium nuclei in the TRISO fuel particles split when they absorb a neutron, and the graphite slows the new neutrons so the chain reaction carries on. The fission heat passes to helium flowing through the core, which leaves at 925°C. The reactor runs at 178.5MWth of its 210MWth rating.
   notes: ['The design basis is a generic 925°C HTGR. No commercial reactor runs at this outlet, and Xe-100 is only a labelled analogue with a much lower outlet temperature.']
   figs: {'rot': ('925', 'fixed'), 'rating': ('210', 'input'), 'output': ('178.5', 'live'), 'surplus': ('31.5', 'live')}
 step 2 | Hot primary helium at 925°C passes 121.7MW to a separate secondary loop in the intermediate heat exchanger (IHX), so the reactor coolant never enters the chemical plant. The circulator sends the cooled helium back to the core.
   notes: ['A branch of the hot helium also runs to the power block, which step 12 follows.', 'Input outside its evidence range: IHX approach at 25 K lies outside the evidence range 50 to 82 K, and the cross rules cap the control at 50 K.']
   figs: {'ihx': ('121.7', 'live'), 'he': ('79.2', 'live'), 'e': ('3.96', 'live'), 'app': ('25', 'input')}
 step 3 | The secondary helium leaves the IHX at 900°C, heats the reformer tubes and returns at 466°C, bringing 120.8MWth of process heat that a conventional reformer would raise by burning natural gas. A second circulator on the return leg draws 3.48MWe; the drawing does not show it.
   notes: ["The helium connections on the reformer are drawn schematically. In the engine's tube model the hot helium enters at the process gas outlet end and flows against the process gas.", 'Input outside its evidence range: Helium supply temperature is fixed at 900 C. Highest measured secondary helium temperature is 859 C, HTTR 2004 high-temperature run (IAEA NP-T-4.3 printed p.14).']
   figs: {'sup': ('900', 'fixed'), 'ret': ('466', 'fixed'), 'q': ('120.8', 'live'), 'he': ('54.0', 'live')}
 step 4 | Natural gas at 23.2MMSCFD and process steam enter the reformer, joined by the methane-rich recycle of step 9. In the catalyst tubes methane and steam react to hydrogen and carbon monoxide, taking up the helium's heat. The engine sets the exit at 871°C, INL's equilibrium assumption; with helium at 900°C, the tube model reaches only 782.1°C (precomputed) on the assumed tube.
   notes: []
   figs: {'ng': ('23.2', 'live'), 'out': ('871', 'fixed'), 'tube': ('782.1', 'pre')}
 step 5 | The syngas raises steam as it leaves the reformer and enters the high-temperature shift bed at 335°C. Over the shift catalyst, carbon monoxide and steam form more hydrogen and carbon dioxide, and the beds release 8.1MW of heat.
   notes: ['The drawing shows the two beds, and the coolers between them, as one vessel.']
   figs: {'hts': ('335', 'fixed'), 'lts': ('191', 'fixed'), 'q': ('8.1', 'live')}
 step 6 | The shifted gas is cooled in steps, and 8.4MW of its heat is hot enough to help drive the MEA reboiler of step 10. In the separator at 40°C, 40t/h of water condenses out before the gas goes on to the absorber.
   notes: ['The engine models six exchangers where the drawing shows one cooler.']
   figs: {'ex4': ('8.4', 'live'), 'sep': ('40', 'fixed'), 'cond': ('40', 'live')}
 step 7 | In the packed absorber the gas rises against falling 30wt% aqueous monoethanolamine (MEA) at 26.8bar. The amine takes up 90.0% of the CO₂ in one pass and leaves at the bottom as rich solvent; the hydrogen-rich gas leaves at the top.
   notes: []
   figs: {'pp': ('90.0', 'live'), 'cap': ('1,098', 'live'), 'sol': ('894', 'live'), 'p': ('26.8', 'input')}
 step 8 | The hydrogen-rich gas, with 10,214kg/h of hydrogen, crosses the dashed boundary to pressure swing adsorption (PSA), an external block the engine does not simulate. At an assumed 88.0% recovery it delivers 8,988kg/h of purified hydrogen, 66,925t/y at 85.0% availability.
   notes: ['The two PSA beds are drawn schematically, since the block is external and has no model.']
   figs: {'feed': ('10,214', 'live'), 'prod': ('8,988', 'live'), 'ty': ('66,925', 'live'), 'rec': ('88.0', 'fixed')}
 step 9 | The PSA reject, still holding 1,226kg/h of hydrogen, crosses back into the boundary and is compressed for the cryogenic unit. The cold separation returns hydrogen to the PSA feed and a methane-rich recycle to the reformer. The nitrogen-rich cut is burned and leaves by the stack as the plant's residual 38,309t/y of CO₂.
   notes: ["CO₂ slip into this unit is about ten times INL's, and freeze-out and reverse shift are unquantified, so INL's recycle may not transfer to MEA.", 'The burner of the nitrogen-rich cut is not drawn; the stack stands for it.']
   figs: {'tc': ('472', 'live'), 'rc': ('355', 'live'), 'bc': ('117', 'live'), 'atm': ('38,309', 'live')}
 step 10 | Rich solvent is warmed in the rich/lean exchanger and stripped at 2.0bar by reboiler steam, which drives the CO₂ out; the lean solvent is cooled and pumped back to the absorber. The reboiler needs 51.0MW, met in a fixed order: freed Selexol steam 12.7MW, export steam 16.8MW, syngas heat 8.4MW, and reactor steam 13.1MW last.
   notes: ["No line on the drawing brings heat into the reboiler; the order comes from the engine's energy balance."]
   figs: {'q': ('51.0', 'live'), 'srd': ('3.8', 'input'), 'gap': ('13.1', 'live'), 'ps': ('2.0', 'input')}
 step 11 | The condenser returns water from the stripper overhead as reflux, and the CO₂ is compressed to 138.9bar (precomputed) using 4.25MWe. Then 359,777t/y of CO₂ leaves by ship for storage offshore abroad, which the model treats as a tariff of 60S$/t with no simulation.
   notes: ['How offshore storage abroad counts as abatement is unresolved.']
   figs: {'cap': ('359,777', 'live'), 'e': ('4.25', 'live'), 'p': ('138.9', 'pre'), 'cost': ('21.6', 'live')}

   figs: {'feed': ('10,214', 'live'), 'prod': ('8,988', 'live'), 'ty': ('66,925', 'live'), 'rec': ('88.0', 'fixed')}
 step 9 | The PSA reject, still holding 1,226kg/h of hydrogen, crosses back into the boundary and is compressed for the cryogenic unit. The cold separation returns hydrogen to the PSA feed and a methane-rich recycle to the reformer. The nitrogen-rich cut is burned and leaves by the stack as the plant's residual 38,309t/y of CO₂.
   notes: ["CO₂ slip into this unit is about ten times INL's, and freeze-out and reverse shift are unquantified, so INL's recycle may not transfer to MEA.", 'The burner of the nitrogen-rich cut is not drawn; the stack stands for it.']
   figs: {'tc': ('472', 'live'), 'rc': ('355', 'live'), 'bc': ('117', 'live'), 'atm': ('38,309', 'live')}
 step 10 | Rich solvent is warmed in the rich/lean exchanger and stripped at 2.0bar by reboiler steam, which drives the CO₂ out; the lean solvent is cooled and pumped back to the absorber. The reboiler needs 51.0MW, met in a fixed order: freed Selexol steam 12.7MW, export steam 16.8MW, syngas heat 8.4MW, and reactor steam 13.1MW last.
   notes: ["No line on the drawing brings heat into the reboiler; the order comes from the engine's energy balance."]
   figs: {'q': ('51.0', 'live'), 'srd': ('3.8', 'input'), 'gap': ('13.1', 'live'), 'ps': ('2.0', 'input')}
 step 11 | The condenser returns water from the stripper overhead as reflux, and the CO₂ is compressed to 138.9bar (precomputed) using 4.25MWe. Then 359,777t/y of CO₂ leaves by ship for storage offshore abroad, which the model treats as a tariff of 23S$/t with no simulation.
   notes: ['How offshore storage abroad counts as abatement is unresolved.']
   figs: {'cap': ('359,777', 'live'), 'e': ('4.25', 'live'), 'p': ('138.9', 'pre'), 'cost': ('8.3', 'live')}
 step 12 | A branch of the hot primary helium carries 59.6MWth to the power block, whose turbine makes 19.8MWe at 39.7% efficiency and sends 13.1MW of steam heat to the reboiler. The helium circulators draw 7.45MWe of the 19.78MWe site demand, and the grid supplies 0.00MWe.
   notes: ["Whether INL's power conversion efficiency is already net of primary circulation is unverified, so this load may be counted twice."]
   figs: {'br': ('59.6', 'live'), 'en': ('19.8', 'live'), 'et': ('19.78', 'live'), 'imp': ('0.00', 'live')}
 step 13 | End to end, the plant makes 66,925t/y of purified hydrogen and sends 359,777t/y of CO₂ to storage. Against the 616,361t/y an unabated reformer would emit on INL's baseline, its residual of 38,309t/y leaves a net abatement of 578,052t/y. Part of that is the CO₂ stored; the rest is never formed, since no natural gas is burned for reformer heat. The abatement target of 250,000t/y is met at these inputs. On Cho's and IEAGHG's baselines the net abatement is 587,436t/y and 563,745t/y.
   notes: ['The plant sits on the proposed Western Island site, a proposal of this study with no official approval.']
   figs: {'h2': ('66,925', 'live'), 'cap': ('359,777', 'live'), 'atm': ('38,309', 'live'), 'ab': ('578,052', 'live')}

 step 1 | Inside the core, uranium nuclei in the TRISO fuel particles split when they absorb a neutron, and the graphite slows the new neutrons so the chain reaction carries on. Scenario: Plant basis: Case A, INL reference; PSA reject fate: Export across the boundary. The drawing shows the headline plant, Case B with the reject recycled, so some drawn lines may carry nothing here. End to end, the plant makes 97,946t/y of purified hydrogen and sends 545,112t/y of CO₂ to storage. Against the 902,060t/y an unabated reformer would emit on INL's baseline, its residual of 218,077t/y leaves a net abatement of 683,984t/y. Part of that is the CO₂ stored; the rest is never formed, since no natural gas is burned for reformer heat. The abatement target of 250,000t/y is not met at these inputs. On Cho's and IEAGHG's baselines the net abatement is 697,719t/y and 663,045t/y.
   notes: ['The plant sits on the proposed Western Island site, a proposal of this study with no official approval.']

===== rating=100 feasible False
 step 1 | Not feasible at these inputs. Infeasible: the process needs 178.5 MWth and the rating is 100.0 MWth, a deficit of 78.5 MWth. 32.0 MWth of heat duty sits above the rating, and grid import supplies electricity only. Inside the core, uranium nuclei in the TRISO fuel particles split when they absorb a neutron, and the graphite slows the new neutrons so the chain reaction carries on. The fission heat passes to helium flowing through the core, which leaves at 925°C. The plant needs 178.5MWth and the reactor is rated at 100MWth.
   notes: ['The design basis is a generic 925°C HTGR. No commercial reactor runs at this outlet, and Xe-100 is only a labelled analogue with a much lower outlet temperature.']
   figs: {'rot': ('925', 'fixed'), 'rating': ('100', 'input'), 'output': ('100.0', 'live'), 'surplus': ('-78.5', 'live')}
 step 2 | Not feasible at these inputs.  step 4 | Natural gas at 23.2MMSCFD and process steam enter the reformer, joined by the methane-rich recycle of step 9. In the catalyst tubes methane and steam react to hydrogen and carbon monoxide, taking up the helium's heat. The engine sets the exit at 871°C, INL's equilibrium assumption; with helium at 900°C, the tube model reaches only 782.1°C (precomputed) on the assumed tube.

 step 5 | The syngas raises steam as it leaves the reformer and enters the high-temperature shift bed at 335°C. Over the shift catalyst, carbon monoxide and steam form more hydrogen and carbon dioxide, and the beds release 8.1MW of heat.
   notes: ['The drawing shows the two beds, and the coolers between them, as one vessel.']

 step 6 | The shifted gas is cooled in steps, and 8.4MW of its heat is hot enough to help drive the MEA reboiler of step 10. In the separator at 40°C, 40t/h of water condenses out before the gas goes on to the absorber.

 step 7 | In the packed absorber the gas rises against falling 30wt% aqueous monoethanolamine (MEA) at 26.8bar. The amine takes up 90.0% of the CO₂ in one pass and leaves at the bottom as rich solvent; the hydrogen-rich gas leaves at the top.
   notes: []

 step 8 | The hydrogen-rich gas, with 10,214kg/h of hydrogen, crosses the dashed boundary to pressure swing adsorption (PSA), an external block the engine does not simulate. At an assumed 88.0% recovery it delivers 8,988kg/h of purified hydrogen, 66,925t/y at 85.0% availability.
   notes: ['The two PSA beds are drawn schematically, since the block is external and has no model.']

 step 9 | The PSA reject, still holding 1,226kg/h of hydrogen, crosses back into the boundary and is compressed for the cryogenic unit. The cold separation returns hydrogen to the PSA feed and a methane-rich recycle to the reformer. The nitrogen-rich cut is burned and leaves by the stack as the plant's residual 38,309t/y of CO₂.
   notes: ["CO₂ slip into this unit is about ten times INL's, and freeze-out and reverse shift are unquantified, so INL's recycle may not transfer to MEA.", 'The burner of the nitrogen-rich cut is not drawn; the stack stands for it.']
   figs: {'tc': ('472', 'live'), 'rc': ('355', 'live'), 'bc': ('117', 'live'), 'atm': ('38,309', 'live')}
 step 10 | Rich solvent is warmed in the rich/lean exchanger and stripped at 2.0bar by reboiler steam, which drives the CO₂ out; the lean solvent is cooled and pumped back to the absorber. The reboiler needs 51.0MW, met in a fixed order: freed Selexol steam 12.7MW, export steam 16.8MW, syngas heat 8.4MW, and reactor steam 13.1MW last.
   notes: ["No line on the drawing brings heat into the reboiler; the order comes from the engine's energy balance."]
